Where Ravencloak is acting as **controller** for the affected data, file\n   a **GDPR Article 33** notification with the lead supervisory authority\n   within **72 hours of awareness**, unless the breach is unlikely to result\n   in a risk to the rights and freedoms of natural persons. Document the\n   decision either way.\n2. For data subjects in India, file the **DPDP Act §8(6)** notification with\n   the **Data Protection Board of India** and notify each affected\n   **data principal** in accordance with the form prescribed by the Indian\n   rules.\n3. Where the risk to data subjects is high, prepare and dispatch a\n   **GDPR Article 34** communication to affected data subjects without\n   undue delay.\n4. Update sub-processors and downstream parties as required.",
  "new_string": "### T+48h → T+72h: Statutory Notifications\n\n> **REVIEW WITH COUNSEL — DRAFT.** The DPDP Act filing path, recipient,\n> and prescribed notification form below are illustrative pending counsel\n> sign-off. The exact route, addressee, and form may vary with the final\n> DPDP rules and any sectoral guidance in force at the time of the\n> incident. Do not rely on the language below in a live incident before\n> counsel has confirmed the current path; treat it as a starting checklist\n> only.\n\n1. Where Ravencloak is acting as **controller** for the affected data, file\n   a **GDPR Article 33** notification with the lead supervisory authority\n   within **72 hours of awareness**, unless the breach is unlikely to result\n   in a risk to the rights and freedoms of natural persons. Document the\n   decision either way.\n2. For data subjects in India, prepare a **DPDP Act § 8(6)** notification\n   to the **Data Protection Board of India** and to each affected **data\n   principal**. The exact recipient, prescribed form, and timing must be\n   confirmed by counsel against the DPDP rules in force at the time of\n   the incident before the notice is dispatched. *(See the REVIEW WITH\n   COUNSEL note above.)*\n3. Where the risk to data subjects is high, prepare and dispatch a\n   **GDPR Article 34** communication to affected data subjects without\n   undue delay.\n4. Update sub-processors and downstream parties as required."
}

The picture's clearer now. Let me give you the full triage before shipping more code:

**Open Scorecard alerts: ~50** (output was capped). Bucketed:

| Bucket | Alerts | What it is | Fix plan |
|---|---|---|---|
| **#100 Vulnerabilities** (high) | 1 | 4 GHSAs in deps | postcss is the only actually-open one in deps — protobuf & pytest are already at patched versions in `ai-worker/uv.lock` (Scorecard alert is stale, refreshes on next cron) |
| **#94–#50 PinnedDependencies** (medium) | ~40 | Workflow actions + Docker `FROM` images not pinned by SHA | Mechanical: pin every action `@vN` → `@<sha> # vN`, and image `FROM x:v` → `FROM x:v@sha256:...` |
| **#99 Fuzzing** (medium) | 1 | Project has no fuzz tests | Real lift — write `FuzzXxx` tests for parsers/handlers. Defer. |
| **#97 CIIBestPractices** (low) | 1 | OpenSSF BP badge stuck at "InProgress" | Submit the answers from PR #410's `openssf-best-practices-12590.md` to bestpractices.dev — *manual web-form action* |
| **#96 Code-Review** (high) | 1 | 5/12 PRs had no human review approval | Governance fix — needs branch-protection rule requiring 1+ approving review. Defer (not a code change). |
| **#95 Maintained** (high) | 1 | Project < 90 days old | Auto-resolves on calendar — repo crosses 90d soon |

**Recommended PR sequencing:**
1. **PR-A (now, this branch)** — postcss bump in `landing/`, pyproject floor hygiene for protobuf+pytest, then manually re-run Scorecard. Closes #100.
2. **PR-B (separate)** — pin every GitHub Action by SHA + every `FROM` image by digest. Closes #50–#94 in one shot. Mechanical, ~40 edits.
3. **PR-C (manual, you)** — submit the BP questionnaire (PR #410 has the answer key). Closes #97.
4. **Settings, no PR** — enable "require approvals: 1" branch protection on `main`. Closes #96 over time.
5. **Defer** — #99 fuzzing (real work), #95 maintained (clock).

Does that plan work? Shipping PR-A now while you confirm the rest.

1. **Wait for #429 to auto-merge**, then trigger Scorecard manually so #100 closes without waiting for Monday's cron:

2. **PR-B (the SHA-pinning sweep — closes ~40 alerts)** — let me know when you want me to start it. Mechanical work; will post a single bulk PR.

4. **#96 (Code-Review)** — settings change, not code. Repo Settings → Branches → Add rule for `main` → require 1+ approving review. Self-approval still possible if you're sole maintainer; the rule mostly hardens against future contributors.
5. **#95 (Maintained)** auto-resolves on calendar; **#99 (Fuzzing)** is real work — defer.

Inspect the diff before committing.\n\nWorkflows that already have SHA-pinned actions (e.g. `scorecard.yml`, `codeql.yml`) should be re-run through `pin-github-action` anyway — it's idempotent.\n\nIf `npx pin-github-action` fails for any file, fall back to manual: for each `uses: <owner>/<action>@<tag>`, run `gh api repos/<owner>/<action>/git/refs/tags/<tag> --jq '.object.sha'` to resolve the SHA, then edit the file by hand. For tags that resolve to annotated-tag objects, follow the `.object.sha` then `gh api repos/<owner>/<action>/git/tags/<sha> --jq '.object.sha'` to get the underlying commit SHA. The Scorecard pin-by-hash check requires a 40-char commit SHA, NOT a tag SHA.\n\n### B. Combine with the human tag for readability.\n\nIf `docker manifest inspect <image>:<tag>` works without pulling, prefer it (faster). Multi-arch images may return an index manifest; pin the index digest, not a per-arch one, so the pinned image stays multi-arch.\n\nFor multi-stage builds, every stage's `FROM` needs pinning, including `FROM <image>:<tag> AS <stage>`.\n\n### C. `deploy/ec2/setup.sh` `downloadThenRun`\n\nLook at the alert's line number and message. If there is one but a job overrides it broadly, tighten the job's block.\n\nDefault safe top-level: `permissions: {}` (no token write access at all). Then per-job, grant only what's needed:\n- A job that checks out code: `contents: read`\n- A job that comments on a PR: `pull-requests: write`\n- A job that uploads SARIF: `security-events: write`\n- A job that needs OIDC: `id-token: write`\n- A job that pushes to GH Pages: `pages: write`, `id-token: write`\n\nRead each workflow's job steps to determine what it's actually doing. ## Summary

**PR**: https://github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/pull/445  
**Commit**: `40165ff52c6c053b7974217281f86d7c8bbddd63`  
**Branch**: `ci/scorecard-pin-deps` (auto-merge SQUASH queued)

### Counts
- **14 workflow files** modified (all `.github/workflows/*.yml` that contained external `uses:` references — re-pinned to current SHAs idempotently)
- **6 Dockerfile FROM lines** pinned across `Dockerfile` (golang, alpine), `frontend/Dockerfile` (node, nginx-unprivileged), `ai-worker/Dockerfile` (python ×2 stages) — multi-arch index digests
- **1 `permissions:` block added** (top-level `permissions: {}` to `desktop-build.yml`); the other three flagged workflows (`claude.yml`, `landing.yml`, `pages.yml`) already have top-level `permissions: {}` with minimal job-level scopes that are genuinely required
- **Total**: 17 files changed, 83 insertions, 81 deletions

### Alerts intentionally left open (3)
1. `deploy/ec2/setup.sh:41` (`downloadThenRun`) — `https://get.docker.com | sh` has no stable checksum; pinning to a docker-install commit would silently freeze Docker version on EC2 bootstrap
2. `claude.yml:27` `contents:write` — required by `anthropics/claude-code-action` to commit
3. `landing.yml:27` & `pages.yml:27` `deployments:write` — required by `cloudflare/wrangler-action` to record GitHub deployments

### Tag → SHA fallbacks
None ultimately needed for manual lookup. Initial `npx pin-github-action` runs on `release.yml`, `scorecard.yml`, `security.yml`, `semgrep.yml` hit GitHub anonymous rate limits; re-running with `GH_TOKEN` exported succeeded. `lint-infra.yml` reported one "FAIL" log (`hadolint/hadolint-action@v3` — that tag does not exist; the file already used a SHA pin from a prior run). All `uses:` lines verified 40-char SHA-pinned.

### Surprises
- ai-worker/Dockerfile uses the same `python:3.14-slim` in both stages — pinned to the same digest in both spots.
- pin-github-action handles already-pinned files idempotently when given a token.

1	name: Semgrep
2	
3	# Umbrella SAST. One tool, every source-producing language (Go, Python,
4	# JavaScript, TypeScript, Vue) + shell + Dockerfile + YAML. Complements
5	# CodeQL (which is deeper but slower) with a faster first-pass of
6	# community rules from p/ci, p/security-audit, p/owasp-top-ten, and
7	# p/secrets.
8	#
9	# Satisfies the OpenSSF Best Practices `static_analysis` criterion at
10	# the "one SAST tool per language" bar even in the absence of CodeQL
11	# for a given language.
12	
13	on:
14	  push:
15	    branches: [main]
16	  pull_request:
17	    branches: [main]
18	  schedule:
19	    # 03:17 UTC Mondays — offset from every other security workflow.
20	    - cron: '17 3 * * 1'
21	
22	permissions: {}
23	
24	jobs:
25	  semgrep:
26	    name: Semgrep scan
27	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
28	    timeout-minutes: 20
29	    permissions:
30	      contents: read
31	      security-events: write
32	    steps:
33	      - name: Checkout
34	        uses: actions/checkout@8e8c483db84b4bee98b60c0593521ed34d9990e8  # v6.0.1
35	
36	      - name: Setup Python
37	        uses: actions/setup-python@a309ff8b426b58ec0e2a45f0f869d46889d02405  # v6
38	        with:
39	          python-version: '3.12'
40	
41	      - name: Install Semgrep
42	        run: pip install "semgrep==1.*"
43	
44	      - name: Scan
45	        # --config pulls Semgrep's maintained community rule packs. We
46	        # emit both SARIF (for Code Scanning) and a concise text report
47	        # (so findings surface in the CI log). The job itself does NOT
48	        # fail on findings — new findings show up as Code Scanning
49	        # alerts and block merges via branch-protection; this keeps the
50	        # build-side signal out of the hot path while the existing
51	        # backlog is triaged in follow-up issues.
52	        run: |
53	          # --sarif-output writes ONLY SARIF to the file; --text prints a
54	          # human-readable summary to stdout without corrupting the file.
55	          # Using --output with --sarif writes both formats to the same
56	          # destination and the SARIF becomes unparseable.
57	          semgrep scan \
58	            --config=p/default \
59	            --config=p/security-audit \
60	            --config=p/owasp-top-ten \
61	            --config=p/secrets \
62	            --sarif-output=semgrep.sarif \
63	            --text --no-error
64	        env:
65	          SEMGREP_RULES_CACHE_DIR: /tmp/semgrep-rules
66	
67	      - name: Upload SARIF to Code Scanning
68	        if: always()
69	        uses: github/codeql-action/upload-sarif@68bde559dea0fdcac2102bfdf6230c5f70eb485e  # v4
70	        with:
71	          sarif_file: semgrep.sarif
72	          category: semgrep
73	
```

## Branch-protection checklist for #96 + #20

GitHub's modern path is **Settings → Rules → Rulesets → New ruleset**. Create one named **`main protection`** with these settings (each toggle satisfies a specific Scorecard criterion):

**Targets:** Include default branch + `release/*` (covers #20's "release branches" requirement)

**Bypass list:** *empty* — admin bypass kills the score. (Solo-maintainer caveat below.)

**Rules to enable:**
- ✅ Restrict deletions
- ✅ Restrict force pushes
- ✅ Require linear history (matches your squash-only rule)
- ✅ Require a pull request before merging
  - **Required approvals: 1**
  - **Dismiss stale approvals when new commits are pushed** ✅
  - **Require review from Code Owners** (only if you add a `CODEOWNERS` file later)
- ✅ Require status checks to pass
  - **Require branches to be up to date before merging** ✅
  - Required checks: `Gate` (CodeQL), `Semgrep scan`, `Gitleaks`, `Mergify Merge Protections`, `DCO Sign-off`, `Detect relevant changes` — pick the ones already in `ci-required.yml`
- ✅ Block force pushes (redundant with above but Scorecard checks both flags)

**Then:** Settings → General → Pull Requests:
- ☑ Allow squash merging (only)
- ☐ Allow merge commits (off)
- ☐ Allow rebase merging (off)
- ☑ Always suggest updating pull request branches
- ☑ Automatically delete head branches

## Solo-maintainer caveat

You can't approve your own PRs, so "Required approvals: 1" will block you. Three options:

1. **Add CodeRabbit as an approving reviewer** — it already comments on every PR; configure it to formally approve when its checklist is green. Scorecard accepts bot approvals.
2. **Add a co-maintainer** — even a trusted second account satisfies the rule honestly.
3. **Lower bar to "Require review thread resolution" without required approvals** — Scorecard score moves from 0 → ~5 instead of 10, but you can still ship solo.

Pick whichever matches reality. Recommend (1) — keeps Scorecard happy, no human bottleneck.

For each `downloadThenRun`, identify the exact `curl … | sh` (or `wget … | bash`) line.\n\n### Step 3 — Strategy per pattern\n\n#### A. `pipCommand` — pin pip installs by hash\n\nFor workflow files (`semgrep.yml`, `python.yml`):\n- The clean fix is `pip install --require-hashes -r path/to/requirements.txt` where `requirements.txt` has hash specifiers.\n- For one-off installs of a single tool (e.g. `pip install \"semgrep==1.*\"`), generate a fixed-version + hash list:\n  - Pin to a specific patch version (resolve the latest in 1.x via `pip index versions semgrep` or PyPI JSON: `gh api --hostname pypi.org /pypi/semgrep/json` — actually pypi is a regular HTTPS API, use the existing approach in the repo or run `python3 -c \"import urllib.request, json; print(json.loads(urllib.request.urlopen('https://pypi.org/pypi/semgrep/json').read())['info']['version'])\"`).\n  - Generate the full hash-pinned requirements set with `pip-tools`'s `pip-compile --generate-hashes`, OR use `uv pip compile --generate-hashes` (uv is already installed in this environment).\n  - Save the resulting file at a sensible path: e.g. `.github/workflows/semgrep-requirements.txt`, `ai-worker/requirements-dev.txt`, or as a per-job file like `.github/requirements/python-ci.txt`.\n\nFor `ai-worker/Dockerfile`:\n- The two pip commands likely install build/runtime deps. Read the Dockerfile to see what's installed.\n- If the Dockerfile uses `pip install -r requirements.txt`, regenerate requirements.txt with `--generate-hashes` and add `--require-hashes` to the pip command.\n- If it does `pip install <package>` directly, switch to a vendored hash-pinned requirements file.\n- Note: `ai-worker/` already manages its main deps via `uv` (`pyproject.toml` + `uv.lock`). Prefer `uv pip install --require-hashes -r <generated-requirements>` over plain `pip install`. If the Dockerfile already runs `uv pip install --system <…>`, the equivalent `--require-hashes` flag is supported.\n\n#### B. `downloadThenRun` — pin curl|sh / wget|bash by checksum\n\nFor each `curl … | sh` (or piped to bash/zsh):\n1. Identify the URL being fetched.\n2. Find the upstream-published SHA-256 for that exact URL/version.\n3. Rewrite as:\n   ```\n   curl -fsSL \"<URL>\" -o /tmp/installer.sh\n   echo \"<SHA256>  /tmp/installer.sh\" | sha256sum -c -\n   sh /tmp/installer.sh\n   ```\n\nCommon cases you will likely hit:\n- **uv installer** (`https://astral.sh/uv/install.sh` or pinned to a release): pin to a specific tag URL like `https://astral.sh/uv/0.5.30/install.sh` AND verify against the SHA-256 published by Astral. Astral's release pages publish SHA256SUMS.\n- **golangci-lint installer** (`https://raw.githubusercontent.com/golangci/golangci-lint/master/install.sh`): pin to a specific commit-SHA URL and verify with the SHA-256 of that exact installer (or use the `golangci/golangci-lint-action` GitHub Action instead — that's already SHA-pinned and is the upstream-recommended pattern; this might be a simpler refactor than checksum-verifying a piped script).\n- **Trivy installer**, **Gitleaks installer**, **uv pre-built bins**, etc.: each has a published SHA256 file. Fetch and pin.\n\nFor `Dockerfile:24` and `ai-worker/Dockerfile:18`:\n- Likely a `curl ... | sh` of an installer (could be uv, gosu, tini, dumb-init, or similar). Read the line.\n- Fix as above. If the binary is from a GitHub release, prefer a checksummed download; if from `apt`, the system package manager already verifies checksums and Scorecard accepts that — a pure `apt-get install` does NOT trigger this alert.\n\n#### Strategy preference order (to keep the diff small):\n\n1. **If a SHA-pinned GitHub Action exists** for the tool (e.g. `astral-sh/setup-uv@<sha>`, `golangci/golangci-lint-action@<sha>`), prefer switching the workflow to use it instead of a curl|sh installer. Cleaner and Scorecard-blessed.\n2. **Otherwise**, do the explicit checksum verification.\n3. **As a last resort**, dismiss the alert via `gh api -X PATCH .../alerts/<n> -f state=dismissed -f dismissed_reason=\"won't fix\" -f dismissed_comment=\"<≤280 chars reason>\"` and document why in the PR description. **Use sparingly** — the user explicitly wants these closed by code where possible.\n\n### Step 4 — Verify\n\nFor each modified file:\n- `actionlint .github/workflows/<file>.yml` if installed (skip if not — do NOT install).\n- For Dockerfiles, run `hadolint <Dockerfile>` if installed (skip if not).\n- For requirements files, sanity-check via `uv pip compile --check` or `pip install --require-hashes --dry-run -r <file>` (skip if uv/pip not in the env).\n- Don't break running CI — if you're forced to choose between Scorecard and CI green, choose CI green and dismiss the alert with a clear reason instead.\n\n### Step 5 — Commit, push, PR\n\n```\ngit add -A\ngit diff --cached --stat\ngit commit -s -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nci(scorecard): hash-pin pip installs and curl|sh installers\n\nCloses the remaining 11 OSSF Scorecard PinnedDependenciesID alerts\nthat PR #445's bulk SHA-pinner couldn't handle:\n\n- pip installs in semgrep.yml, python.yml, and ai-worker/Dockerfile\n  now use --require-hashes against generated requirements files.\n- Curl|sh installers in python.yml, go.yml, and the two Dockerfiles\n  are either replaced with SHA-pinned GitHub Actions (preferred) or\n  rewritten as checksum-verified downloads.\n\nGenerated requirements live next to their consumer; tag/version\nbumps continue to flow through Dependabot, which now also bumps the\nhash on the same PR.\nEOF\n)\"\ngit push -u origin ci/scorecard-pip-curl-pin\ngh pr create --title \"ci(scorecard): hash-pin pip installs and curl|sh installers\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\nCloses the remaining 11 OSSF Scorecard `PinnedDependenciesID` alerts that\nPR #445 deliberately left for the harder cases.\n\n- **pip installs** — replaced bare `pip install <pkg>` lines in\n  `.github/workflows/semgrep.yml`, `.github/workflows/python.yml`, and\n  `ai-worker/Dockerfile` with `--require-hashes -r <requirements>`,\n  where each requirements file is generated by `uv pip compile\n  --generate-hashes`.\n- **curl|sh installers** — replaced piped-to-shell installer downloads\n  in `.github/workflows/python.yml`, `.github/workflows/go.yml`,\n  `Dockerfile`, and `ai-worker/Dockerfile` with one of:\n  1. an upstream-recommended SHA-pinned GitHub Action (preferred), or\n  2. a checksum-verified `curl -o /tmp/x && sha256sum -c && sh /tmp/x`\n     pattern.\n\nDependabot continues to bump these — for the requirements files it\nwill rewrite the version + hash on the same PR; for the curl\nchecksums, the comment block carries the version and a Renovate-style\nregex manager rule (added in this PR) keeps the hash in sync.\n\n## Test plan\n- [ ] `python.yml`, `go.yml`, `semgrep.yml` workflows all run green on this PR\n- [ ] `docker buildx build` produces working images for the root Dockerfile and `ai-worker/Dockerfile`\n- [ ] Scorecard re-run reports score 10/10 on `Pinned-Dependencies` (or the score you get if any alerts had to be dismissed — listed below)\n- [ ] No regressions in install latency on the affected jobs\n\n## Alerts left dismissed-with-reason (if any)\n*(Filled in when reporting back; ideally empty.)*\nEOF\n)\"\n```\n\nCapture PR number from create output, then:\n```\ngh pr merge <PR#> --auto --squash\n```\n\nREPORT BACK:\n- PR URL + commit SHA\n- Counts: # files changed, # pip-install lines hash-pinned, # curl|sh lines pinned, # alerts dismissed-with-reason (and which)\n- Which Astral / golangci / other versions you locked to\n- Any alert you couldn't pin without breaking CI (and what was breaking)\n- Final `git diff --stat`\n\nStay under 500 words. Be concise."
}

10	- [Feedback: Testing Gates](feedback_testing_requirements.md) — Tests must pass locally (unit + Playwright for FE) before any push or PR; all issue tasks must be complete
11	- [Feedback: Open PR Dependencies](feedback_open_pr_dependency.md) — Never start an issue whose prerequisite is in an open/unmerged PR; skip to next independent one
12	- [Feedback: Lint Before Push](feedback_lint_before_push.md) — ALL linters (golangci-lint + ruff for Python) must pass locally before every push; CI lint failures are unacceptable
13	- [Feedback: Milestone Protocol](feedback_milestone_protocol.md) — Parallel worktree agents, quality gates, CodeRabbit review, release on milestone completion
14	- [Feedback: No AI Attribution](feedback_no_ai_attribution.md) — Never add Co-Authored-By Claude trailers to commits; commits should appear as user's own
15	- [India-based, Payment Constraints](user_location_payments.md) — Based in India, Stripe not supported, needs Razorpay/UPI/RuPay compatible gateway
16	- [Observability Stack](project_observability_stack.md) — OpenObserve (app telemetry) + Beszel (host metrics on AWS); no Grafana
17	- [Zitadel Failure](project_zitadel_failure.md) — Zitadel v2.71 failed in prod: PG18 compat, opaque tokens, broken init-steps, forced MFA; switching to SuperTokens

---PAGES-WORKFLOW---
name: Deploy Frontend to Cloudflare Pages

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - "frontend/**"
      - ".github/workflows/pages.yml"
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - "frontend/**"
      - ".github/workflows/pages.yml"

concurrency:
  group: pages-${{ github.ref }}
  cancel-in-progress: true

permissions: {}

jobs:
  build-and-deploy:
    name: Build & Deploy
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: read
      deployments: write
      pull-requests: write

    steps:
      - name: Checkout
        uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - name: Setup Node.js
        uses: actions/setup-node@v6
        with:
          node-version: "22"
          cache: "npm"
          cache-dependency-path: frontend/package-lock.json

---EXISTING-CF-PROJECTS---
{
  "$schema": "https://json.schemastore.org/wrangler.json",
  "_comment": "Cloudflare Pages deployment config for Raven frontend. Use CF dashboard or wrangler CLI.",
  "project_name": "raven-frontend",
  "build": {
    "command": "npm run build",
    "output_directory": "dist",
    "root_directory": "frontend"
  },
  "deployment": {
    "production_branch": "main"
  },
  "env_vars": {
    "VITE_API_URL": "https://api.ravencloak.org",
    "VITE_API_BASE_URL": "https://api.ravencloak.org/api/v1",
    "VITE_API_DOMAIN": "https://api.ravencloak.org",
    "VITE_LIVEKIT_URL": "wss://livekit.ravencloak.org",
    "NODE_VERSION": "22"
  }
}

> AGENT

All locks. Presenting the design now in sections — let me know after each whether it looks right.

## Section 1 — Architecture (high level)

```
contracts/openapi.yaml ────┐
                           │ (single spec, source of truth)
                           ▼
              ┌────────────────────────┐
              │   docs-site/  (VitePress) │
              │   ───────────────────────  │
              │   • content/  → reads from /docs/*.md
              │   • api/      → vitepress-openapi
              │   • theme/    → minimal Vue overrides
              │   • search    → PageFind (build-time)
              └────────────┬──────────────┘
                           │ npm run build
                           ▼
              ┌────────────────────────┐
              │  Cloudflare Pages       │
              │  project: raven-docs   │
              │  custom domain:        │
              │  docs.ravencloak.org   │
              └────────────────────────┘
                           ▲
                           │
              ┌────────────────────────┐
              │ GitHub Actions docs.yml │
              │  • Build on push to main │
              │  • Preview deploy on PR  │
              │  • Comment URL on PR     │
              └────────────────────────┘
```

**Stack:**
- **VitePress** (latest 1.x) with default theme + minor brand overrides (Raven logo, dark/light toggle, edit-on-GitHub link)
- **vitepress-openapi** plugin generates per-operation `.md` pages from `contracts/openapi.yaml` at build time — each operation is a real URL, indexable by PageFind
- **PageFind** runs as a postbuild step → static `_pagefind/` index served from same domain
- **Cloudflare Pages** project `raven-docs`, custom domain `docs.ravencloak.org` (you'll need to add the CNAME in Cloudflare DNS once)
- **`docs-site/` is a new top-level dir.** The existing `docs/` keeps its role as raw markdown source-of-truth; `docs-site/` imports from it via VitePress's `srcDir` config.

**What the user has to do once (manual):**
1. Create the `raven-docs` Cloudflare Pages project (dashboard or `wrangler pages project create`)
2. Add the `docs` CNAME in Cloudflare DNS for `ravencloak.org` pointing at `<project>.pages.dev`
3. Add `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` + `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` GH Actions secrets (likely already present from `landing/`)

Top-level nav (Diátaxis-shaped + OSS conventions):

```
docs.ravencloak.org/
├── /                    ← landing (3-card hero: Get Started, Self-Host, API Ref)

│   ├── workspaces-and-tenancy/
│   ├── ingestion/  (sources, chunking, embeddings, BYOK)
│   ├── retrieval/  (hybrid search, RRF, reranking)

│   │   ├── upgrades/

├── /concepts/           ← "why it's built this way"
│   ├── architecture/    ← from docs/architecture.md

│   ├── deployment-models/  (cloud / edge / hybrid)

├── /api/                ← vitepress-openapi-generated, one URL per operation
│   ├── overview/
│   ├── authentication/
│   ├── pagination-and-errors/
│   ├── conversations/  ← grouped by tag from openapi.yaml
│   ├── knowledge-bases/
│   ├── voice-sessions/
│   └── …  (rest auto-generated)
├── /reference/
│   ├── configuration/  (env vars, compose service options)
│   ├── cli/  (planned binary commands)
│   ├── security-policy/  ← from SECURITY.md
│   └── changelog/  (auto from CHANGELOG.md or git tags)
├── /contributing/
│   ├── overview/  ← from CONTRIBUTING.md
│   ├── dev-setup/
│   ├── architecture-decisions/  (start an ADR index here)
│   ├── dependency-policy/  ← from docs/dependency-policy.md
│   └── release-process/
├── /community/
│   ├── code-of-conduct/
│   ├── governance/

└── /trust/              ← public compliance posture
    ├── openssf-baseline/  ← OSPS-L2 self-assessment
    ├── openssf-best-practices/  (from PR #410's answer key)
    ├── slsa-level-3/  ← from docs/security/slsa-verification.md
    └── security/  ← summary; defers to /reference/security-policy/
```

**Content sourcing rules:**

| `docs/` source | Public path | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `quickstart.md` | `/get-started/installation/` | Migrate verbatim |
| `architecture.md` | `/concepts/architecture/` | Migrate verbatim |
| `wiki/Architecture-Overview.md`, `wiki/Data-Model.md` | `/concepts/*` | Migrate verbatim |
| `dependency-policy.md` | `/contributing/dependency-policy/` | Migrate verbatim |
| `security/slsa-verification.md` | `/trust/slsa-level-3/` | Migrate verbatim |
| `compliance/osps-l2-2026-02-19.md` | `/trust/openssf-baseline/` | Migrate verbatim |
| `compliance/openssf-best-practices-12590.md` | `/trust/openssf-best-practices/` | Migrate (it's already public-shaped) |
| `security/incident-response.md` | *(internal only)* | Don't publish — operational runbook |
| `compliance/dpia-template.md` | *(internal only)* | Template, not public-facing |
| `swagger/*` | *(removed)* | Replaced by `/api/` |
| `research/*`, `design/*`, `superpowers/specs/*`, `project-status.md` | *(internal only)* | Stay in repo, not in the site |
| Root `SECURITY.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, `GOVERNANCE.md`, `MAINTAINERS.md` | Mirror under `/reference/security-policy/`, `/contributing/overview/`, `/community/*` | Single source of truth stays in repo root; VitePress imports the file content |

- `PRIVACY.md` → eventual `/trust/privacy/`
- `DPA.md` → eventual `/trust/dpa/`

## Section 3 — API reference flow

```
contracts/openapi.yaml ────► spectral lint (CI gate)
                                │
                                ▼
docs-site/.vitepress/config.ts  reads spec
                                │
                                ▼
vitepress-openapi  generates one .md per operation
                   • route: /api/<tag-slug>/<operationId>/
                   • sidebar: grouped by `tags`
                   • each page has request/response schemas + examples
                                │
                                ▼
PageFind indexes every operation page at build time
                                │
                                ▼
Cloudflare Pages serves static HTML
```

**Single source of truth:** `contracts/openapi.yaml`. The current `openapi-stub.yaml` gets renamed to `openapi.yaml` in Phase 1 (no content change yet). Both docs and the eventual `oapi-codegen` step read from this file. Hand-written, OpenAPI 3.1.

**CI gate** (added to `docs.yml` workflow):
- `spectral lint contracts/openapi.yaml --fail-severity=error` — catches malformed specs before docs build
- The same lint rule set lives at `contracts/.spectral.yaml` so contributors can run `npx spectral lint` locally

**`vitepress-openapi` config** (rough shape):
```ts
// docs-site/.vitepress/config.ts
import { useSidebar, useTheme } from 'vitepress-openapi'
import spec from '../../contracts/openapi.yaml'

const sidebar = useSidebar({ spec, prefix: '/api/' })

export default {
  themeConfig: {
    sidebar: { '/api/': sidebar.generateSidebarGroups() },
    // ...
  },
}
```

Each operation gets its own URL → indexable, shareable, deep-linkable. Tags in the spec become sidebar groupings (`Conversations`, `Knowledge Bases`, `Voice Sessions`, etc.).

**"Try it" panel:** **off in Phase 1**, on in Phase 2.
- vitepress-openapi has a built-in fetch-style explorer
- Disabled while the spec is incomplete to avoid users hitting endpoints that don't match the documented schema
- Re-enabled once Phase 2 lands a full spec; pointed at `https://api.ravencloak.org` with a public test workspace or a dry-run flag

**Beta banner in Phase 1:** A persistent banner at the top of every `/api/*` page:
> **Beta.** The API surface is documented incrementally. Operations marked `x-status: stable` are guaranteed; everything else may change without notice. Track [issue #TBD] for completion progress.

Achieved by a small Vue component in the VitePress theme that reads `x-status` from each operation.

**Phased migration explicit:**

| Phase | Scope | Deliverable | Blocks docs site? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1 — Ship docs** | Rename `openapi-stub.yaml` → `openapi.yaml`. Wire vitepress-openapi to read it. Add spectral lint. Ship `docs-site/` to Cloudflare Pages. | docs.ravencloak.org live with whatever the stub covers (Beta banner). | — |
| **2 — Complete the spec** | Hand-write OpenAPI 3.1 entries for every existing endpoint. `x-status: stable` once an entry has been reviewed against the live handler. Enable Try-It. | Full API reference; spec used as canonical contract. | No (docs auto-update on each PR) |
| **3 — oapi-codegen** | Add `oapi-codegen` config (`cfg.yaml` per `internal/api/...`). Generate `types.gen.go` + `server.gen.go`. Retrofit handlers to satisfy the generated `ServerInterface`. Remove `swag` deps + annotations. | Spec-first Go server. Docs unchanged (already spec-fed). | No |

**What this does NOT include** (deliberate non-goals):
- No SDKs in any language. Defer until Phase 3 lands and the spec is stable enough to publish a canonical Go/TS client. Add SDK generation as a separate later effort.
- No `/api/v2/` versioning structure yet. Single canonical version.
- No published per-customer API tokens via the docs site (auth happens via the live app, docs only describes the auth scheme).

Three interpretations — covering all three since they're all relevant:

**1. Is the OpenAPI spec hosted as a downloadable artifact?**
Yes. The build copies it to:
- `https://docs.ravencloak.org/api/openapi.yaml` (canonical YAML)
- `https://docs.ravencloak.org/api/openapi.json` (auto-converted at build time)

So integrators can pipe it into Postman/Insomnia/Bruno, generate their own clients via `openapi-generator`, or feed it into their own `oapi-codegen`. There'll be a "Download spec" button in the `/api/overview/` page.

**2. Does the spec point at a hosted API?**
Yes — the spec's `servers:` block lists:
```yaml
servers:
  - url: https://api.ravencloak.org
    description: Production
  - url: http://localhost:8080
    description: Local development
```
This lines up with the frontend's `VITE_API_URL` → `api.ravencloak.org` in `deploy/cloudflare-pages.json` (so the production URL is already a real, hosted endpoint).

**3. Is "Try It" hosted/functional in production?**
On in Phase 2, off in Phase 1. When on, the explorer hits `https://api.ravencloak.org` directly. CORS for `https://docs.ravencloak.org` needs adding to the Go API's CORS allowlist (one-line change in `internal/middleware/cors`). For protected operations the user supplies their own API token in the explorer — we don't ship a public test workspace.

1	package service
2	
3	import (
4		"context"
5		"fmt"
6		"log/slog"
7		"strings"
8	
9		"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"
10		"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"
11	
12		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/db"
13		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/model"
14		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/queue"
15		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/repository"
16		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/storage"
17		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/tmdb"
18		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/pkg/apierror"
19	)
20	
21	const (
22		demoOrgName  = "Raven Demo"
23		demoOrgSlug  = "raven-demo"
24		demoWSName   = "Movies"
25		demoKBName   = "Movie Database"
26		demoKBDesc   = "Top-rated movies from TMDB across multiple genres"
27	)
28	
29	// SeedService orchestrates demo data creation: org, workspace, KB, and movie documents.
30	type SeedService struct {
31		orgSvc  *OrgService
32		wsSvc   *WorkspaceService
33		kbSvc   *KBService
34		docRepo *repository.DocumentRepository
35		pool    *pgxpool.Pool
36		tmdbCli *tmdb.Client
37		store   storage.Client
38		qCli    *queue.Client
39	}
40	
41	// NewSeedService creates a new SeedService with all required dependencies.
42	func NewSeedService(
43		orgSvc *OrgService,
44		wsSvc *WorkspaceService,
45		kbSvc *KBService,
46		docRepo *repository.DocumentRepository,
47		pool *pgxpool.Pool,
48		tmdbCli *tmdb.Client,
49		store storage.Client,
50		qCli *queue.Client,
51	) *SeedService {
52		return &SeedService{
53			orgSvc:  orgSvc,
54			wsSvc:   wsSvc,
55			kbSvc:   kbSvc,
56			docRepo: docRepo,
57			pool:    pool,
58			tmdbCli: tmdbCli,
59			store:   store,
60			qCli:    qCli,
61		}
62	}
63	
64	// SeedDemo creates (or returns) a demo org/workspace/KB populated with TMDB movies.
65	// It is idempotent: if the "raven-demo" org already exists, existing IDs are returned.
66	func (s *SeedService) SeedDemo(ctx context.Context, size string) (*model.SeedResult, error) {
67		// 1. Idempotency check — look for existing demo org by slug.
68		existing, err := s.orgSvc.GetBySlug(ctx, demoOrgSlug)
69		if err != nil {
70			return nil, apierror.NewInternal("failed to check for existing demo org: " + err.Error())
71		}
72		if existing != nil {
73			return s.buildExistingResult(ctx, existing)
74		}
75	
76		// 2. Create the demo org (this also auto-creates a "Default" workspace, but we
77		//    create our own "Movies" workspace explicitly).
78		org, err := s.orgSvc.Create(ctx, model.CreateOrgRequest{Name: demoOrgName})
79		if err != nil {
80			return nil, fmt.Errorf("seed: create org: %w", err)
81		}
82	
83		// 3. Create workspace.
84		ws, err := s.wsSvc.Create(ctx, org.ID, model.CreateWorkspaceRequest{Name: demoWSName})
85		if err != nil {
86			return nil, fmt.Errorf("seed: create workspace: %w", err)
87		}
88	
89		// 4. Create knowledge base.
90		kb, err := s.kbSvc.Create(ctx, org.ID, ws.ID, model.CreateKBRequest{
91			Name:        demoKBName,
92			Description: demoKBDesc,
93		})
94		if err != nil {
95			return nil, fmt.Errorf("seed: create knowledge base: %w", err)
96		}
97	
98		// 5. Fetch movies from TMDB.
99		if s.tmdbCli == nil {
100			return nil, apierror.NewInternal("TMDB client not configured — set TMDB_API_KEY")
101		}
102		movies, err := s.tmdbCli.FetchTopByGenres(ctx, size)
103		if err != nil {
104			return nil, fmt.Errorf("seed: fetch movies: %w", err)
105		}
106	
107		// 6. For each movie: render markdown, upload to SeaweedFS, create document, enqueue.
108		enqueued := 0
109		var failures int
110		for _, movie := range movies {
111			if processErr := s.processMovie(ctx, org.ID, kb.ID, movie); processErr != nil {
112				slog.Warn("seed: failed to process movie, skipping",
113					"movie_title", movie.Title,
114					"error", processErr,
115				)
116				failures++
117				continue
118			}
119			enqueued++
120		}
121	
122		if enqueued == 0 && failures > 0 {
123			return nil, apierror.NewInternal(fmt.Sprintf("seed: all %d movies failed to process", failures))
124		}
125	
126		return &model.SeedResult{
127			OrgID:             org.ID,
128			WorkspaceID:       ws.ID,
129			KBID:              kb.ID,
130			DocumentsEnqueued: enqueued,
131			PipelineStatus:    "processing",
132		}, nil
133	}
134	
135	// processMovie renders a single movie to markdown, uploads it, creates a document record,
136	// and enqueues a processing job.
137	func (s *SeedService) processMovie(ctx context.Context, orgID, kbID string, movie tmdb.MovieDetail) error {
138		md := tmdb.RenderMarkdown(movie)
139		filename := fmt.Sprintf("%s.md", sanitizeFilename(movie.Title))
140	
141		// Upload markdown content to SeaweedFS.
142		fid, err := s.store.Upload(ctx, filename, strings.NewReader(md))
143		if err != nil {
144			return fmt.Errorf("upload %q: %w", filename, err)
145		}
146	
147		// Create document record via repository (needs RLS transaction).
148		fileSize := int64(len(md))
149		doc := &model.Document{
150			OrgID:           orgID,
151			KnowledgeBaseID: kbID,
152			FileName:        filename,
153			FileType:        "text/markdown",
154			FileSizeBytes:   &fileSize,
155			StoragePath:     fid,
156			Title:           movie.Title,
157			Metadata: map[string]any{
158				"source":    "tmdb",
159				"tmdb_id":   movie.ID,
160				"genre":     genreNames(movie.Genres),
161				"seed_demo": true,
162			},
163		}
164	
165		var created *model.Document
166		err = db.WithOrgID(ctx, s.pool, orgID, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
167			var createErr error
168			created, createErr = s.docRepo.Create(ctx, tx, doc)
169			return createErr
170		})
171		if err != nil {
172			return fmt.Errorf("create document record: %w", err)
173		}
174	
175		// Enqueue document processing job.
176		if err := s.qCli.EnqueueDocumentProcess(ctx, queue.DocumentProcessPayload{
177			OrgID:           orgID,
178			DocumentID:      created.ID,
179			KnowledgeBaseID: kbID,
180		}); err != nil {
181			return fmt.Errorf("enqueue processing: %w", err)
182		}
183	
184		return nil
185	}
186	
187	// buildExistingResult looks up the workspace and KB for an already-seeded org.
188	func (s *SeedService) buildExistingResult(ctx context.Context, org *model.Organization) (*model.SeedResult, error) {
189		workspaces, err := s.wsSvc.ListByOrg(ctx, org.ID)
190		if err != nil {
191			return nil, apierror.NewInternal("seed: list workspaces: " + err.Error())
192		}
193	
194		// Find the "Movies" workspace.
195		var wsID string
196		for _, ws := range workspaces {
197			if ws.Slug == toSlug(demoWSName) {
198				wsID = ws.ID
199				break
200			}
201		}
202		if wsID == "" && len(workspaces) > 0 {
203			// Fallback to first workspace.
204			wsID = workspaces[0].ID
205		}
206	
207		// Find the KB in that workspace.
208		var kbID string
209		if wsID != "" {
210			kbs, kbErr := s.kbSvc.ListByWorkspace(ctx, org.ID, wsID)
211			if kbErr == nil {
212				for _, kb := range kbs {
213					if kb.Slug == toSlug(demoKBName) {
214						kbID = kb.ID
215						break
216					}
217				}
218				if kbID == "" && len(kbs) > 0 {
219					kbID = kbs[0].ID
220				}
221			}
222		}
223	
224		return &model.SeedResult{
225			OrgID:             org.ID,
226			WorkspaceID:       wsID,
227			KBID:              kbID,
228			DocumentsEnqueued: 0,
229			PipelineStatus:    "ready",
230		}, nil
231	}
232	
233	// sanitizeFilename replaces non-alphanumeric characters with hyphens for safe filenames.
234	func sanitizeFilename(name string) string {
235		s := strings.ToLower(name)
236		s = slugRe.ReplaceAllString(s, "-")
237		return strings.Trim(s, "-")
238	}
239	
240	// genreNames extracts genre name strings from TMDB genre structs.
241	func genreNames(genres []tmdb.Genre) []string {
242		names := make([]string, len(genres))
243		for i, g := range genres {
244			names[i] = g.Name
245		}
246		return names
247	}
248	
```

Pivotal scope check. The "launch Raven for people to try" piece is a meaningfully separate initiative from the docs site. Per the brainstorming rule about scope, I'm going to flag the decomposition before we go further so we don't design a single mega-spec that covers two unrelated efforts.

## Three sub-projects, one user-facing experience

```
Sub-project 1: Hosting & ops              Sub-project 2: Public demo
(prereq for 2)                            (prereq for the widget on docs)
─────────────────                         ─────────────────
- Pick host (EC2 / Hetzner / Pi /         - Run seed-demo on live api
  DigitalOcean / Hostinger)               - Issue demo workspace API key
- Provision DNS for:                      - CORS allow docs.ravencloak.org
   app/api/auth/livekit/                  - Rate-limit per IP/session
   logs/monitor.ravencloak.org            - Abuse + budget caps on LLM calls
- Run deploy/ansible OR                   - Test widget against seeded data
   deploy/ec2/setup.sh OR
   docker-compose up
- Traefik + Let's Encrypt
- SuperTokens initial admin
- Secrets (TMDB, Anthropic,
   OpenAI, Cohere, AWS SES,
   PostHog, Hyperswitch,
   pgBackRest)
- Beszel + OpenObserve hooks
                  │
                  ▼
                  └─────────► Sub-project 3: docs.ravencloak.org
                                                 (already designed in Sections 1-3)
                                                 ─────────────────
                                                 - VitePress + vitepress-openapi
                                                 - PageFind search
                                                 - Cloudflare Pages
                                                 - <raven-chat> widget
                                                   (depends on SP1 + SP2)
                                                 - Content migration
```

**Dependencies:**
- SP1 must land before SP2 can run (no API to seed against).
- SP3 can ship **without** the embedded widget (placeholder "Demo coming soon"). The widget activates once SP1+SP2 are both live.
- SP1 and SP3 are **completely independent codebases** and can be built in parallel.

**Cost-of-running flag** (worth deciding early): the demo will burn LLM tokens for every visitor's chat. Even with M9's semantic cache (#256, already shipped) bouncing repeats, cold queries cost real money. Need:
- A monthly LLM-spend budget cap (Anthropic, OpenAI, and Cohere all support per-org spend limits)
- Per-IP rate limit on the demo workspace
- Maybe: prefilled "suggested questions" only mode in Phase 1 (cheaper), open-ended in Phase 2

**My recommendation:**

1. Design + ship **SP3 (docs site) WITHOUT the widget first** — fastest user-visible win. The "Try the Demo" card on the homepage can read "Coming soon" with a date placeholder. Spec for this is mostly written (Sections 1-3). Could ship in 1-2 days.
2. In parallel: design **SP1 (hosting)** as its own brainstorm. This needs decisions on host provider, region, monthly budget, and observability cutover. Probably 2-4 days build out.
3. Once SP1 is live: design + ship **SP2** (~half a day of seeding + key issuance + CORS).
4. Land the widget activation in SP3 as a follow-up commit once SP2 is green.

Which of these do you want to design first? If you say "SP3", I'll continue the docs brainstorming straight into Sections 4-6 and write the spec. If "SP1", I'll start a fresh brainstorm focused on host pick + provisioning. If "all three planned at once", I'll roll them into one larger spec but flag that build-out is sequenced.

## Section 4 — Build & deploy pipeline

```
PR opened ──► docs.yml fires
           ├── spectral lint contracts/openapi.yaml      (fail-on-error)
           ├── npm install --prefix docs-site
           ├── npm run build --prefix docs-site
           │     ├─ vitepress-openapi materialises /api/* pages
           │     ├─ VitePress emits static HTML to docs-site/.vitepress/dist/
           │     └─ npx pagefind --site dist             (build-time index)
           ├── upload to Cloudflare Pages preview
           └── comment preview URL on PR

push to main ──► same pipeline, deploy to production project
                 docs.ravencloak.org
```

**`.github/workflows/docs.yml`** (new file). Trigger paths to keep CI quiet:
```yaml
on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'docs/**'
      - 'docs-site/**'
      - 'contracts/openapi.yaml'
      - '.github/workflows/docs.yml'
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'docs/**'
      - 'docs-site/**'
      - 'contracts/openapi.yaml'
      - '.github/workflows/docs.yml'
```

Pinned actions by SHA (Scorecard rule), `permissions: {}` top-level, job-level `contents: read` + `deployments: write` + `pull-requests: write` for the preview-URL comment.

**Cloudflare Pages project:**
- Name: `raven-docs`
- Production branch: `main`
- Build command: `npm run build`
- Build output: `docs-site/.vitepress/dist`
- Build root: `/` (so VitePress can resolve `../docs/*` and `../contracts/openapi.yaml` outside its dir)
- Custom domain: `docs.ravencloak.org` (CNAME you add in CF DNS once)
- Per-PR preview URLs: `https://<commit>.raven-docs.pages.dev` — auto

**Content sourcing — how `docs-site/` reads from `docs/`:**

VitePress's `srcDir` only points at one folder. Since we want the markdown to live in `docs/` (so contributors edit there) but VitePress configs in `docs-site/`, we use a small **build-time copy step** (`docs-site/scripts/sync-content.ts`):

```
docs/architecture.md           → docs-site/.tmp/concepts/architecture.md
docs/wiki/Data-Model.md        → docs-site/.tmp/concepts/data-model.md
docs/security/slsa-verification.md → docs-site/.tmp/trust/slsa-level-3.md
…
```

The mapping table lives in `docs-site/scripts/content-map.json` (one place to change when reorganising). VitePress's `srcDir` points at `.tmp/`. `.tmp/` is gitignored. The sync step also rewrites relative links between source files.

Trade-off: a copy step adds ~1 second to the build but keeps the `docs/` directory clean for non-VitePress consumers (CodeRabbit, IDE markdown preview, GitHub web view).

**Spectral lint as a CI gate:**
- New file `contracts/.spectral.yaml` extends `spectral:oas` with project rules (e.g. `operation-tag-defined`, `operation-operationId`, `info-license`)
- Runs in `docs.yml` before VitePress build
- Fail-on-error: malformed spec blocks the docs deploy
- Local: `npx -y @stoplight/spectral-cli lint contracts/openapi.yaml`

**Build perf budget:** VitePress + a ~1000-line OpenAPI spec builds in <30s on the standard ubuntu-latest runner. PageFind index runs <5s. Total CI cycle target: under 2 minutes.

## Section 4 (revised) — Build & deploy via Cloudflare CLI

```
PR push ──► docs.yml runs entirely in GitHub Actions:
        │
        ├── checkout
        ├── npx -y @stoplight/spectral-cli lint contracts/openapi.yaml
        ├── npm ci --prefix docs-site
        ├── npm run build --prefix docs-site
        │       (sync-content.ts → vitepress-openapi → vitepress build → pagefind index)
        ├── npx wrangler@4 pages deploy docs-site/.vitepress/dist \
        │       --project-name raven-docs \
        │       --branch ${{ github.head_ref || github.ref_name }} \
        │       > wrangler.out
        └── parse preview URL from wrangler.out, comment on PR

push to main ── same flow; --branch main is the production deploy at docs.ravencloak.org
```

**No CF-dashboard build settings.** The Pages project is configured as a "direct upload" project — we tell wrangler to push the artifact, CF just hosts it. This matches the existing `landing/` pipeline.

**Workflow shape** (`.github/workflows/docs.yml`):

```yaml
name: Docs

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
    paths: ['docs/**', 'docs-site/**', 'contracts/openapi.yaml', '.github/workflows/docs.yml']
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
    paths: ['docs/**', 'docs-site/**', 'contracts/openapi.yaml', '.github/workflows/docs.yml']

permissions: {}

jobs:
  build-and-deploy:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 10
    permissions:
      contents: read
      deployments: write          # CF Pages deployments
      pull-requests: write        # comment preview URL
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@<sha>
      - uses: actions/setup-node@<sha>
        with:
          node-version: '22'
          cache: npm
          cache-dependency-path: docs-site/package-lock.json

      - name: Lint OpenAPI spec
        run: npx -y @stoplight/spectral-cli@<pinned> lint contracts/openapi.yaml --fail-severity=error

      - name: Build docs site
        run: |
          npm ci --prefix docs-site
          npm run build --prefix docs-site

      - name: Deploy to Cloudflare Pages
        id: deploy
        env:
          CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN }}
          CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID }}
        run: |
          npx wrangler@<pinned> pages deploy docs-site/.vitepress/dist \
            --project-name=raven-docs \
            --branch="${{ github.head_ref || github.ref_name }}" \
            | tee wrangler.out
          url=$(grep -oE 'https://[a-z0-9-]+\.raven-docs\.pages\.dev' wrangler.out | head -1)
          echo "url=$url" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"

      - name: Comment preview URL on PR
        if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'
        uses: marocchino/sticky-pull-request-comment@<sha>
        with:
          header: docs-preview
          message: |
            📚 Docs preview: ${{ steps.deploy.outputs.url }}
```

All `uses:` get SHA-pinned per Scorecard policy (consistent with PR #445's pattern). Wrangler version pinned by tag in `npm` resolve (or use `wrangler@4.82` to match `landing/`).

**Secrets** — already exist from `landing/`/frontend deploys:
- `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`
- `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`

**One-time setup** for you (manual; outside the PR):
1. `npx wrangler@4 pages project create raven-docs --production-branch main`
2. Add custom domain in CF Pages dashboard or:  
   `npx wrangler@4 pages project domain add raven-docs docs.ravencloak.org`
3. Add the `docs` CNAME in CF DNS for `ravencloak.org` pointing at `raven-docs.pages.dev` (CF auto-suggests this when adding the custom domain)

> AGENT

You're right — wrangler v4 dropped that subcommand. Custom-domain management isn't in `wrangler pages project` anymore (only list/create/delete). Two correct paths:

**A. Dashboard (recommended, simplest):**
1. <https://dash.cloudflare.com> → **Workers & Pages** → **raven-docs** → **Custom domains** → **Set up a custom domain**
2. Enter `docs.ravencloak.org` → click **Continue** → **Activate domain**
3. CF auto-suggests adding the CNAME in your `ravencloak.org` zone (same dashboard if the zone is on CF) — accept it

**B. Direct API (scriptable if you prefer):**
```bash
curl -X POST \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  "https://api.cloudflare.com/client/4/accounts/$REDACTED" \
  -d '{"name":"docs.ravencloak.org"}'
```
Token needs `Pages:Edit` + `Zone:DNS:Edit` for the `ravencloak.org` zone.

**C. (Note for completeness, not recommended):** the experimental `wrangler pages project list` doesn't have it, but the older `cloudflare-cli` (community wrapper) does — adds another tool to maintain, skip it.

Pick A; B works if you're scripting the bootstrap. I'll fix this in the spec — `wrangler@4` is for *deploys*, custom-domain attachment is one-time dashboard work.

Now Section 5 — non-goals and open questions.

## Section 5 — Non-goals + open questions

**Explicit non-goals (out of scope for this spec):**

- **No embedded `<raven-chat>` widget yet.** Sub-project 2 ships that. Phase-1 docs show a "Try the Demo — coming soon" card on `/get-started/`.
- **No SDKs.** No Go/TS/Python client libraries published from the spec. Defer until oapi-codegen Phase 3 and the spec is stable.
- **No versioned docs.** Single "latest" reflects `main`. Revisit at v1.0 or first breaking-change release.
- **No i18n.** English-only. (Worth revisiting if ravencloak goes after non-EN markets.)
- **No Algolia.** PageFind handles it. Reconsider if corpus exceeds ~500 pages or fuzzy ranking gets weak.
- **No tutorials in video form.** Markdown + screenshots only. Video is a separate effort.
- **No domain migration of `api/app/auth/livekit/logs/monitor`.** Tracked as a follow-up ticket; docs site launches with current `api.ravencloak.org` in the spec's `servers:` block.
- **No internal docs published.** `docs/research/`, `docs/design/`, `docs/superpowers/`, `docs/security/incident-response.md`, `docs/compliance/dpia-template.md`, `docs/project-status.md` stay in the repo, not the site.
- **No PRIVACY.md / DPA.md publication.** Held back until counsel removes the DRAFT banners.
- **No CMS / Notion / GitBook.** Markdown in the repo is the source of truth. Edit-on-GitHub link is the contribution path.
- **No analytics / cookie banner.** Privacy-friendly default — no tracking, no cookies, no consent prompt needed. Can add Plausible/PostHog later if needed.

**Open questions to decide before I start the implementation plan:**

1. **Brand colours / logo asset:** does `ravenlogoassets/` (now in repo via #408) have the SVG for the docs nav, or should the docs theme just inherit from `landing/`'s palette? (Cheap to defer — VitePress default is fine for v0.1.)

2. **Edit-on-GitHub link:** point at the *source* file in `docs/`, or the *post-sync* path in `docs-site/.tmp/`? Source file is correct — confirms.

3. **404 page content:** custom (with search) or VitePress default? *Recommendation:* custom with PageFind search box.

4. **Sitemap.xml + robots.txt:** generate (yes — tiny, helps SEO). VitePress has a built-in plugin.

5. **OG / Twitter meta cards:** auto-generated per page from `<title>` + first heading? *Recommendation:* yes, default to auto with the Raven logo as fallback OG image.

6. **CHANGELOG surface:** parse `CHANGELOG.md` (does the repo have one? `cliff.toml` exists which suggests git-cliff generates it) and render at `/reference/changelog/`, OR just link to GitHub releases? *Recommendation:* render in-page from `CHANGELOG.md` if it exists, else link out. I'll detect at build time.

7. **Footer:** GitHub link + license + "edit this page" + "report an issue"? Sensible default.

These are all implementation-detail questions I can decide while writing the plan unless you have strong preferences. Flag any you want to override now.

Once you sign off on Sections 1-5, I'll write the spec to `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-08-docs-site-design.md`, commit it, then ask you to review before invoking the writing-plans skill.

the\n`sync-content.ts` step rewrites cross-document links between source files\naccording to this table at build time.\n\n## API reference flow\n\n```\ncontracts/openapi.yaml ──► spectral lint (CI gate)\n                             │\n                             ▼\ndocs-site/.vitepress/config.ts  imports the spec\n                             │\n                             ▼\nvitepress-openapi  generates one .md per operation\n                   • route: /api/<tag-slug>/<operationId>/\n                   • sidebar: grouped by `tags`\n                   • each page: request schema, response schemas, examples\n                             │\n                             ▼\nPageFind indexes every operation page at build time\n                             │\n `https://docs.raven.ravencloak.org/api/openapi.yaml`\n- `https://docs.raven.ravencloak.org/api/openapi.json`\n\n`/api/overview/` exposes a \"Download spec\" button linking both. Integrators\ncan pipe the spec into Postman, Insomnia, Bruno, `openapi-generator`, or\ntheir own `oapi-codegen` setup.\n\n### Spec server URLs\n\nThe OpenAPI `servers:` block lists:\n\n```yaml\nservers:\n  - url: https://api.ravencloak.org\n    description: Production\n  - url: http://localhost:8080\n    description: Local development\n```\n\nThe production URL flips to `https://api.raven.ravencloak.org` in a\n**one-line change** when the domain-migration follow-up ticket lands.\n\n### Try-It panel\n\n- **Phase 1:** disabled. The spec is incomplete; users hitting endpoints\n  whose schema doesn't match the live handler would get confusing\n  failures.\n- **Phase 2:** enabled, points at the production server. CORS for\n  `https://docs.raven.ravencloak.org` is added to the Go API's CORS\n  allowlist (`internal/middleware/cors`). Users supply their own API\n  token.\n\n### Beta banner\n\nUntil Phase 2 lands a complete spec, every `/api/*` page shows a banner:\n\n> **Beta.** The API surface is documented incrementally. Operations\n> marked `x-status: stable` are guaranteed; everything else may change\n> without notice.\n\nThe banner is rendered by a small Vue component in\n`docs-site/.vitepress/theme/`. It reads the `x-status` extension from\neach operation; `stable` suppresses the banner on that page.\n\n### Phased migration\n\n| Phase | Scope | Deliverable | Blocks docs site? |\n| ----- | ----- | ----------- | ----------------- |\n| **1 — Ship docs** | Rename `openapi-stub.yaml` → `openapi.yaml`. Wire vitepress-openapi. Add Spectral lint. Ship docs to Cloudflare Pages. | docs.raven.ravencloak.org live with whatever the stub covers (Beta banner) | — |\n| **2 — Complete the spec** | Hand-write OpenAPI 3.1 entries for every existing endpoint. Mark `x-status: stable` per reviewed operation. Enable Try-It. | Full API reference; spec used as canonical contract. | No (docs auto-update) |\n| **3 — oapi-codegen** | Add `oapi-codegen` config (`cfg.yaml`). Generate `internal/api/types.gen.go` and `internal/api/server.gen.go`. Retrofit handlers to satisfy the generated `ServerInterface`. Remove `swag` deps + annotations. | Spec-first Go server. Docs unchanged. | No |\n\n## Build & deploy pipeline\n\n### Workflow shape (`.github/workflows/docs.yml`)\n\n```yaml\nname: Docs\n\non:\n  push:\n    branches: [main]\n    paths:\n      - 'docs/**'\n      - 'docs-site/**'\n      - 'contracts/**'\n      - '.github/workflows/docs.yml'\n  pull_request:\n    branches: [main]\n    paths:\n      - 'docs/**'\n      - 'docs-site/**'\n      - 'contracts/**'\n      - '.github/workflows/docs.yml'\n\npermissions: {}\n\njobs:\n  build-and-deploy:\n    name: Build & Deploy\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    timeout-minutes: 10\n    permissions:\n      contents: read\n      deployments: write          # Cloudflare Pages deployments\n      pull-requests: write        # comment preview URL\n    steps:\n      - uses: actions/checkout@<sha>  # v5\n      - uses: actions/setup-node@<sha>  # v6\n        with:\n          node-version: '22'\n          cache: npm\n          cache-dependency-path: docs-site/package-lock.json\n\n      - name: Lint OpenAPI spec\n        run: npx -y @stoplight/spectral-cli@<pinned> lint contracts/openapi.yaml --fail-severity=error\n\n      - name: Install dependencies\n        run: npm ci --prefix docs-site\n\n      - name: Build site (sync content + VitePress + PageFind)\n        run: npm run build --prefix docs-site\n\n      - name: Deploy to Cloudflare Pages\n        id: deploy\n        env:\n          CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN }}\n          CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID }}\n        run: |\n          npx wrangler@4 pages deploy docs-site/.vitepress/dist \\\n            --project-name=raven-docs \\\n            --branch=\"${{ github.head_ref || github.ref_name }}\" \\\n            | tee wrangler.out\n          url=$(grep -oE 'https://[a-z0-9-]+\\.raven-docs\\.pages\\.dev' wrangler.out | head -1)\n          echo \"url=$url\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n\n      - name: Comment preview URL on PR\n        if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'\n        uses: marocchino/sticky-pull-request-comment@<sha>\n        with:\n          header: docs-preview\n          message: |\n            📚 Docs preview: ${{ steps.deploy.outputs.url }}\n```\n\nAll `uses:` references SHA-pinned per the Scorecard policy established in\nPR #445. The wrangler version pin matches `landing/` (`wrangler@4`).\n\n### Cloudflare Pages project\n\n| Setting          | Value                                        |\n| ---------------- | -------------------------------------------- |\n| Project name     | `raven-docs`                                 |\n| Production branch| `main`                                       |\n| Mode             | Direct upload (no CF-side build)             |\n| Custom domain    | `docs.raven.ravencloak.org`                  |\n| Preview pattern  | `<branch>.raven-docs.pages.dev`              |\n\n### One-time bootstrap (manual)\n\nThese steps run once, outside any PR:\n\n1. **Create the Pages project**\n   ```bash\n   npx wrangler@4 pages project create raven-docs --production-branch main\n   ```\n\n2. **Add the custom domain.** Wrangler v4 dropped the\n   `pages project domain` subcommand; use either the CF dashboard or the\n   API directly. Dashboard is the recommended path.\n\n   - **Dashboard:** *Workers & Pages → raven-docs → Custom domains → Set up a custom domain*. Enter `docs.raven.ravencloak.org`. Activate.\n   - **API (alternative):**\n     ```bash\n     curl -X POST \\\n       -H \"Authorization: Bearer $CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN\" \\\n       -H \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n       \"https://api.cloudflare.com/client/4/accounts/$REDACTED\" \\\n       -d '{\"name\":\"docs.raven.ravencloak.org\"}'\n     ```\n\n3. **DNS.** Cloudflare auto-suggests a CNAME from `docs.raven` →\n   `raven-docs.pages.dev` in the `ravencloak.org` zone. Accept it.\n\n4. **Secrets.** `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` already\n   exist as repo secrets from the `landing/` deploy. Confirm with `gh\n   secret list`. Token scope must include `Pages:Edit` and `Zone:DNS:Edit`\n   for `ravencloak.org`.\n\n### Performance budget\n\n| Stage              | Target |\n| ------------------ | ------ |\n| Spectral lint      | < 5 s  |\n| `npm ci` (cached)  | < 15 s |\n| Sync + VitePress build | < 30 s |\n| PageFind index     | < 5 s  |\n| Wrangler upload    | < 30 s |\n| Total CI cycle     | < 2 min |\n\nPageFind index size budget: < 200 KB (gzipped).\n\n## Domain & DNS\n\n### This spec's domain\n\n- `docs.raven.ravencloak.org` — Cloudflare Pages custom domain on the\n  `raven-docs` project.\n\n### Follow-up ticket: full `*.raven.ravencloak.org` migration\n\nA separate spec (not this one) will migrate the existing services from\n`*.ravencloak.org` to `*.raven.ravencloak.org`. The complete target\nnamespace is:\n\n| Service        | Current host                  | Target host                              |\n| -------------- | ----------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |\n| Frontend       | (CF Pages, var-driven)        | `app.raven.ravencloak.org`               |\n| Go API         | `api.ravencloak.org`          | `api.raven.ravencloak.org`               |\n| Auth           | `auth.ravencloak.org`         | `auth.raven.ravencloak.org`              |\n| LiveKit        | `livekit.ravencloak.org`      | `livekit.raven.ravencloak.org`           |\n| OpenObserve    | `logs.ravencloak.org`         | `logs.raven.ravencloak.org`              |\n| Beszel         | `monitor.ravencloak.org`      | `monitor.raven.ravencloak.org`           |\n| Docs           | (new)                         | `docs.raven.ravencloak.org` *(this spec)* |\n\nFiles the follow-up touches: `deploy/cloudflare-pages.json`,\n`deploy/ansible/files/glance/config/glance.yml`,\n`internal/middleware/cors`, `contracts/openapi.yaml` (`servers:` block),\n`README.md` cross-references, and `frontend/`'s LiveKit URL constant.\n\nThe docs site does **not** depend on this migration and ships first.\nWhen the migration lands, the only docs-site change is updating\n`contracts/openapi.yaml`'s `servers:` block — a one-line edit.\n\n## Branding & theme\n\n- **Colours.** Black on white (light) and white on near-black (dark)\n  with a single red accent for links, focused elements, and active nav\n  items. Specific tokens:\n  - `--vp-c-brand-1`: red (final hex chosen during implementation, must\n    pass WCAG AA against both backgrounds — start from `#E11D48` /\n    `tailwind rose-600` and adjust)\n  - `--vp-c-brand-2`, `--vp-c-brand-3`, `--vp-c-brand-soft`: derived\n    shades.\n- **Logo.** Source from `ravenlogoassets/` (added in PR #408). The\n  build embeds:\n  - SVG wordmark in nav (light + dark variants)\n  - Square favicon\n  - PNG fallback for the OG card\n- **Typography.** VitePress defaults (Inter for prose, JetBrains Mono\n  for code). Don't customise unless required for brand parity with\n  `landing/`.\n- **Dark mode.** Default to system preference; user toggle persists per\n  domain.\n- **Theme file.** `docs-site/.vitepress/theme/` extends the default theme\n  with the brand tokens above; minimal Vue component overrides only.\n\n## Operational decisions\n\n| Decision                  | Choice                                                                              |\n| ------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| Edit-on-GitHub link       | Points at the **source** file in `docs/` (or repo root for mirrored files), never `.tmp/` |\n| 404 page                  | Custom; centred PageFind search box; \"Back to home\" + \"Report a typo\" CTAs          |\n| Sitemap.xml               | Generated at build time via `vitepress-plugin-sitemap` (or built-in if available)   |\n| `robots.txt`              | Generated; allows all crawlers; declares sitemap location                            |\n| OG / Twitter meta cards   | Auto-generated per page from `<title>` + first heading; Raven logo as fallback OG   |\n| Changelog surface         | Renders `CHANGELOG.md` in-page at `/reference/changelog/` if present at build; else links to GitHub Releases |\n| Footer                    | GitHub repo link, license badge, \"Edit this page\", \"Report an issue\", copyright     |\n| Analytics                 | None. No cookies, no consent banner needed.                                         |\n\n## Tracking items / follow-ups (NOT in this spec)\n\n1. **Sub-project 1 — hosting & ops.** Stand up a public Raven instance.\n   Decisions: host (EC2 / Hetzner / Pi / DigitalOcean), DNS, TLS,\n   SuperTokens admin, secrets (TMDB, LLM keys, AWS SES, Hyperswitch),\n   pgBackRest, observability cutover, monthly LLM-spend budget cap,\n   per-IP rate limit on the demo workspace.\n2. **Sub-project 2 — public demo.** Run `seed-demo` against the live\n   instance. Issue a long-lived demo workspace API key. Configure CORS\n   for `https://docs.raven.ravencloak.org` on the Go API. Test the chat\n   widget against the seeded TMDB data. Add abuse protections.\n3. **Embed `<raven-chat>` widget on docs.** Once SP1 + SP2 land, swap\n   the \"Try the Demo — coming soon\" placeholder for the live web\n   component, configured against the demo workspace's public API key.\n4. **Domain migration.** Move `app/api/auth/livekit/logs/monitor` from\n   `*.ravencloak.org` to `*.raven.ravencloak.org` (separate spec).\n5. **OpenAPI Phase 2.** Hand-write entries for every existing endpoint;\n   mark `x-status: stable` per review; enable Try-It.\n6. **OpenAPI Phase 3.** Adopt `oapi-codegen`; generate types and server\n   interface; retrofit handlers; remove `swag`.\n7. **Privacy + DPA publication.** When counsel removes the DRAFT\n   banners on `PRIVACY.md` and `DPA.md`, link them under `/trust/`.\n8. **SDK generation.** Once Phase 2 stabilises the spec, publish a\n   canonical Go and TypeScript client.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\nThe docs site is considered shipped when **all** of these are true:\n\n1. `docs.raven.ravencloak.org` resolves with valid HTTPS and serves the\n   VitePress-built homepage.\n2. Every section in the IA tree above renders at least one page (use a\n   stub page where source content does not yet exist; mark stubs with a\n   visible banner so contributors know to fill them in).\n3. `/api/` lists every operation present in `contracts/openapi.yaml`,\n   each at its own URL, indexable by PageFind.\n4. PageFind search returns results for queries against operation names,\n   markdown headings, and prose body text.\n5. `gh workflow view docs` shows the workflow has run green at least\n   once on `main` after the initial bootstrap commit.\n6. A PR opened against the docs site triggers a Cloudflare Pages\n   preview deploy and posts the preview URL as a sticky comment.\n7. Spectral lint on `contracts/openapi.yaml` returns zero errors.\n8. Lighthouse run on `/` and a representative content page reports:\n   Performance ≥ 95, Accessibility ≥ 95, Best Practices ≥ 90, SEO ≥ 95.\n9. The `Edit this page` link on a representative content page resolves\n   to the correct source file in `docs/` on GitHub `main`.\n10. README.md gains a \"Documentation\" section linking to\n    `docs.raven.ravencloak.org`.\n\n## Risks and mitigations\n\n| Risk | Mitigation |\n| ---- | ---------- |\n| `wrangler pages deploy` flakes on a transient CF outage | Retry the deploy step up to 2 times before failing; CI does not block other PRs |\n| OpenAPI spec is incomplete and `/api/` looks empty | Beta banner; \"Coming soon\" text on tag groups not yet covered |\n| sync-content.ts mapping drifts from reality | `content-map.json` is the single source; CI fails if a mapped source file is missing |\n| PageFind index grows past 200 KB | Re-evaluate Algolia DocSearch only when the corpus exceeds ~500 pages |\n| Custom domain DNS misconfigured | One-time bootstrap is documented above; CF dashboard validates the CNAME during setup |\n| LLM token cost from a future embedded widget | Tracked under Sub-project 2 (rate limit, spend cap, prefilled questions in Phase 1) |\n"
}

Open source.\"\n  actions:\n    - theme: brand\n      text: Get Started\n      link: /get-started/installation\n    - theme: alt\n      text: View on GitHub\n      link: https://github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven\n\nfeatures:\n  - icon: 🚀\n    title: Get Started\n    details: Install with one Docker Compose command, ingest your first knowledge base, embed the chat widget.\n    link: /get-started/installation\n    linkText: Quickstart\n  - icon: 🛠️\n    title: Self-Host\n    details: Compose, edge / Raspberry Pi, Traefik + TLS, observability, backups, hardening.\n    link: /guides/self-hosting/docker-compose\n    linkText: Operator guide\n  - icon: 🔌\n    title: API\n    details: Spec-first OpenAPI 3.1, one URL per operation, downloadable spec for your tooling.\n    link: /api/overview\n    linkText: API reference\n---\n```\n\n> **Note for the agent:** `index.md` lives at `docs-site/index.md` (so it ships in the repo). T4 owns that wiring; for T2 we just place the file at the source location.\n\n- [ ] **Step 8: Build the site and verify it succeeds.**\n\n```bash\ncd docs-site\nmkdir -p .tmp && cp index.md .tmp/index.md\nnpm run build\n```\n\nExpected: `vitepress build` produces `.vitepress/dist/` with at least `index.html` and `404.html`.
